\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}=\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\mathcal{N}(\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle,\sigma^{2})}[\pi^{\ast}(1)+\pi^{\ast}(2)\mathcal{F}_{-y}]
in a previous Lemma;

and for convolution
(f\ast g)(\nu)=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}f(\nu-\nu^{\prime})g(\nu^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\nu^{\prime}
, we can exchange expectation and
convolution.

Let
\nu^{\prime}:=-\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}
, then

\nu^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(-\frac{\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}},\frac{\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)
; and
\nu^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}
, then
\nu^{\prime\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}},\frac{\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)xy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}x\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\mathcal{N}(\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle,\sigma^{2})}[\pi^{\ast}(1)+\pi^{\ast}(2)\mathcal{F}_{-y}]\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}x\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\mathcal{N}(\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle,\sigma^{2})}\left[\pi^{\ast}(1)\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)y-\pi^{\ast}(2)\tanh\left(-\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)y\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\pi^{\ast}(1)\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left[\frac{x\sigma^{2}}{\langle x,\theta\rangle}\right]{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(-\frac{\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}},\frac{\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)}}\tanh(\nu-\nu^{\prime})\nu^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\pi^{\ast}(2)\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left[\frac{x\sigma^{2}}{\langle x,\theta\rangle}\right]{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}},\frac{\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)}}\tanh(\nu-\nu^{\prime\prime})\nu^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sigma\tanh(\nu)\ast\nu\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left[\frac{x}{\langle x,\bar{\theta}\rangle}\right](\pi^{\ast}(1)\mathcal{N}(-\langle x,\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\rangle\langle x,\bar{\theta}\rangle,\langle x,\bar{\theta}\rangle^{2})+\pi^{\ast}(2)\mathcal{N}(\langle x,\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\rangle\langle x,\bar{\theta}\rangle,\langle x,\bar{\theta}\rangle^{2}))

Then, with the previous Lemma of expectaions for 2MLR, we further prove the closed-form expression.
∎

Appendix B
Derivations for EM Update Rules

B.1
.

The negative expected log-likelihood
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
for the mixture model of
s:=(x,y),z\in[M]

with the mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
and regression parameters
\theta
is as follows.

\displaystyle-f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\operatorname{KL}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})||p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]-\mathcal{H}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\log p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]

where
\operatorname{KL}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s}
are KL divengence and Shannon’s
entropy wrt.
s=(x,y)
;

\operatorname{KL}_{z},\mathcal{H}_{z},\operatorname{softmax}_{z}
are KL divengence, Shannon’s
entropy and softmax wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
;

\{q_{s}(z)\mid s\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\}
is a family of distributions wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
,
namely
\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)=1
.

Proof.

Note that
p(s\mid\theta,\pi)=\frac{p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}
, we obtain the following expression.

\displaystyle-f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\operatorname{KL}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})||p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]-\mathcal{H}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\left[\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\left[\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)\log\left(\frac{p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)}{q_{s}(z)}\cdot\frac{q_{s}(z)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\log p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]

∎

B.2
.

The surrogate function
g^{t}
of
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
at
(t-1)
-th iteration
(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})
be expressed as follows.

\displaystyle-g^{t}(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\log p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]\Bigg\}_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}

that is
g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\geq f(\theta,\pi)
, and

g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=f(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}
,

\nabla_{\theta}g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\nabla_{\theta}f(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}
;

where
\operatorname{KL}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s}
are KL divengence and Shannon’s
entropy wrt.
s=(x,y)
;

\operatorname{KL}_{z},\mathcal{H}_{z},\operatorname{softmax}_{z}
are KL divengence, Shannon’s
entropy and softmax wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]

Proof.

Let
r^{t}:=g^{t}-f
, note that
r^{t}=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}\geq 0
, and

\displaystyle r^{t}(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})]_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=0

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}r^{t}}{\mathrm{d}p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}\right]_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{s}(z)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}),(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=0

Hence, the gradients of
r^{t}
wrt.
(\theta,\pi)
at
(t-1)
-th iteration
(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})
are all 0 by the chain rule.
∎

B.3
.

Assuming
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\pi\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}s\mid z
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
; then

\displaystyle Q(\theta,\pi\mid\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\log p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)\right]_{q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log p(x)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)||\pi(z)\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\right]

\displaystyle p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{softmax}_{z}(\log\pi(z)+\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta))

where
\operatorname{KL}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s}
are KL divengence and Shannon’s
entropy wrt.
s=(x,y)
;

\operatorname{KL}_{z},\mathcal{H}_{z},\operatorname{softmax}_{z}
are KL divengence, Shannon’s
entropy and softmax wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
;

q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p\left(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}\right)
and
\pi^{t}=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}},\pi^{t}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}q_{s}(z)
.

Proof.

\bullet

(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
are independent:

therefore
p(z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(z\mid\pi)=\pi(z)

\bullet

\pi\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(s;\theta)\mid z
are conditional independent given
z
:

then
p(\pi\mid z)=p(\pi\mid z;\theta)=p(\pi\mid z,s;\theta)
,
it implies
\frac{p(s,z;\theta,\pi)}{p(z;\theta,\pi)}=\frac{p(s,z;\theta)}{p(z;\theta)}
;

hence
p(s\mid z;\theta,\pi)=p(s\mid z;\theta)
,
p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(s\mid z;\theta,\pi)p(z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(s\mid z;\theta)\pi(z)

\bullet

x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
are independent:

then
p(x\mid z;\theta)=p(x)

hence
p(s\mid z;\theta)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x\mid z;\theta)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x)

therefore
p(s\mid\theta,\pi)=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(x)\cdot\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\pi(z)p(y\mid x,z;\theta)

and
p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)=\frac{p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)}{p(s\mid\theta,\pi)}=\frac{\pi(z)\cdot p(y\mid x,z;\theta)}{\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\pi(z^{\prime})\cdot p(y\mid x,z^{\prime};\theta)}=\operatorname{softmax}_{z}(\log\pi(z)+\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta))

With the above assumptions, we obtain that
p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x)\cdot\pi(z)
, further prove this Lemma.
∎

B.4
.

For MLR
y=\langle x,\theta_{z}^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
,
\theta:=\{\theta_{z}\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}},\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{s}\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left[-\frac{\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle g^{t}(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(2\sigma^{2}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{x}(z)}\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\|\pi(z)\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[q_{s}(z)\right]

where
c=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)
and
q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p\left(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}\right)=\operatorname{softmax}_{z}\left(-\frac{\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z}^{t-1},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi^{t-1}(z)\right)
,
and
\pi^{t}=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}},\pi^{t}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}q_{s}(z)
.

Proof.

Since
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
, then implies
\pi\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}s\mid z

because of
p(\pi\mid z,s)=p(\pi\mid z,x,y)=p(\pi\mid z,x,\varepsilon)=p(\pi\mid z)=\pi(z)
. Hence, we can apply the previous Lemma.

Furthermore,
p(y,x,z;\theta)=p(\varepsilon,x,z;\theta)\left|\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial y}\right|=p(\varepsilon)\cdot p(x,z;\theta)

p(y\mid x,z;\theta)=p(\bm{\varepsilon})=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\varepsilon\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|y-\langle x,\theta_{z}\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{N}(\langle x,\theta_{z}\rangle,\sigma^{2})

Hence, we obtain
\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)=-\frac{\|y-\langle x,\theta_{z}\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+c
, where
c=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)
.

Subsequently, note that
g^{t}(\theta,\pi)=Q(\theta,\pi\mid\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[q_{s}(z)\right]
,
we prove the expression for
g^{t}
by substituting
\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)
in the previous Lemma.

As shown in the proof of the previous Lemma,
p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x)\cdot\pi(z)
.

Hence
p(s\mid\theta,\pi)=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(x)\cdot\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot\pi(z)
,
we prove the expression for
f
by substituting
\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)
in the previous Lemma.
∎

B.5
.

For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.

Then the negative expected log-likelihood
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
and the surrogate function
g^{t}(\theta,\pi)
can be expressed as follows.

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\theta-\theta^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\nu-\nu^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

where
\nu:=\frac{\log\pi(1)-\log\pi(2)}{2}
.

Proof.

Note that
\operatorname{sigmoid}(2t)+\operatorname{sigmoid}(-2t)=1
and

\operatorname{sigmoid}(2t)-\operatorname{sigmoid}(-2t)=\tanh(t)
for
\forall t
,

q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p\left(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}\right)=\operatorname{softmax}_{z}(-\frac{\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z}^{t-1},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi^{t-1}(z))=\operatorname{sigmoid}(2(-1)^{z+1}\left[\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right])
,

\nu^{t-1}:=\frac{\log\pi^{t-1}(1)-\log\pi^{t-1}(2)}{2}
.

\displaystyle\left(2\sigma^{2}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{x}(z)}\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\frac{\|(-1)^{z+1}y-\langle x,\theta\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\frac{y^{2}+\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\frac{yx}{\sigma^{2}},\theta\right\rangle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}

Consider the other terms for
g^{t}
, note
\pi^{t}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}q_{s}(z)
, and

\pi(z)=\operatorname{sigmoid}(2(-1)^{z+1}\nu)
.

Note that
\log 2+\log\cosh\nu=-[\frac{\log\pi(1)+\log\pi(2)}{2}]
and
\operatorname{sigmoid}(2t)-\operatorname{sigmoid}(-2t)=\tanh(t)
for
\forall t
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\|\pi(z)\right]+\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)\log\pi(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log 2+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\cdot\nu

To sum up, we obtain the following.

\displaystyle g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\frac{yx}{\sigma^{2}},\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\log 2+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\cdot\nu

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]

\displaystyle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

For the negative expectation of log-likelihood
f
, we show the following.

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left[-\frac{\|y-\langle\theta_{z},x\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[y^{2}+\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}]-\frac{\log\pi(1)+\log\pi(2)}{2}

\displaystyle-\log 2-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)

\displaystyle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

Note
g^{t}=f
at
(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
, by comparing the expressions
f,g^{t}
,
and use
\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\cosh(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}=\tanh(t)
.

\displaystyle g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\theta-\theta^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\nu-\nu^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

∎

B.6
.

For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.

Then the negative Maximum Likelihood Estimat (MLE)
f_{n}(\theta,\pi):=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\log p(s_{i}\mid\theta,\pi)]
and the surrogate function
g_{n}^{t}(\theta,\pi)
for the dataset
\mathcal{S}:=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
of
n
i.i.d. samples can be expressed as follows.

\displaystyle f_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log p(x_{i})-c

\displaystyle g_{n}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\theta-\theta^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\nu}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\nu-\nu^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log p(x_{i})-c

where
\nu:=\frac{\log\pi(1)-\log\pi(2)}{2}
.

Proof.

This is proved by susbstituting
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n},s_{i}:=(x_{i},y_{i})
for
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})},s:=(x,y)
in the previous Theorem.
∎

B.7
.

(Derivation for Eq. (
3
), (
4
) in Section
2
)
For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}),x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.

The EM update rules
M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1}),N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
for
\theta,\tanh(\nu)
at the population level, namely the minizer of the surrogate
g^{t}
/ the maximizer of
Q
, are the following.

\displaystyle M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Proof.

Take the gradients of
g^{t}
wrt.
\theta,\nu
, we obtain the following.

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta-(\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle\nabla_{\nu}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tanh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Furthermore, the Hessian of
g^{t}
wrt.
\theta,\nu
are positive-definite, we show that the solution to
\nabla_{\theta}g^{t}=0,\nabla_{\nu}g^{t}=0
must be the minimizer of
g^{t}
.

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\theta}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\nu}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cosh^{-2}\nu

Note that
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}=I_{d}
for
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
, we derive the expressions for EM update rules.
∎

B.8
.

(Derivation for Eq. (
5
) in Section
2
)
For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}),x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.

The EM update rules
M_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1}),N_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
for
\theta,\tanh(\nu)
at the finite-sample level, namely the minizer of the surrogate
g_{n}^{t}
, are the following.

\displaystyle M_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle N_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Proof.

This is proved by susbstituting
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n},s_{i}:=(x_{i},y_{i})
for
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})},s:=(x,y)
in the previous Theorem, but note that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\not\equiv I_{d}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof for Results of Population EM Updates

C.1
.

(Theorem
3.1
in Section
3
)
Let
\rho\equiv\rho^{t-1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\bar{\theta}:=\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\sigma},\bar{\theta}^{\ast}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\sigma}
, then the EM update rules for

\theta^{t},\tanh(\nu^{t})
at Population level are as follows.

\displaystyle\theta^{t}:=M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{\sigma}{\pi}\cdot\frac{\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{\left(1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\cosh^{-1}(\nu^{\ast})\right]

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left\{\tanh(\nu)\ast\nu\left[\alpha_{\rho,\|\bar{\theta}\|,\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|,\nu^{\ast}}(\nu)\left(\frac{\frac{\bar{\theta}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}+\hat{e}_{2}\right)+\beta_{\rho,\|\bar{\theta}\|,\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|,\nu^{\ast}}(\nu)\vec{e}_{2}\right]\right\}_{\nu\leftarrow\nu^{t-1}}

\displaystyle\tanh(\nu^{t}):=N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{(1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}}}{\pi\|\bar{\theta}\|\cosh(\nu^{\ast})}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\int}\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\nu^{\prime})K_{0}\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}\cdot\left|\frac{\nu^{\prime}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right|}{[1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}]}\right)\cosh\left(\frac{\rho\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|\left(\frac{\nu^{\prime}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)}{[1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}]}-\nu^{\ast}\right)\mathrm{d}\nu^{\prime}

where
\alpha_{\rho,\|\bar{\theta}\|,\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|,\nu^{\ast}}(\nu):=\cosh\left(\frac{\rho\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|\left(\frac{\nu}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)}{[1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}]}-\nu^{\ast}\right)\cdot K_{0}\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}\cdot\left|\frac{\nu}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right|}{[1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}]}\right)

and
\beta_{\rho,\|\bar{\theta}\|,\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|,\nu^{\ast}}(\nu):=\operatorname{sgn}(\nu)\frac{\sqrt{1+\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|}\sinh\left(\frac{\rho\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|\left(\frac{\nu}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)}{[1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}]}-\nu^{\ast}\right)\cdot K_{1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{1+\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}\cdot\left|\frac{\nu}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right|}{[1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}]}\right)

Proof.

With Lemma in Section
B
, we show that the EM update rules at population level are as follows.

\displaystyle\theta^{t}:=M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle\tanh(\nu^{t}):=N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Then, by using the Lemma for the evaluation of expectations in Section
A
, we complete the proof for these closed-from expression in this Theorem.
∎

C.2
.

(Corollary
3.2
in Section
3
: EM Updates for No Separation Case)
For the special case of no separation of parameters, namely
\operatorname{SNR}:=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}\rightarrow 0
, the EM update
rules for
\theta^{t},\tanh(\nu^{t})
at Population level are

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\theta}^{0}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{0}\|}\cdot\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x-\nu^{t-1})xK_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\tanh(\nu^{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x

where
\bar{\theta}^{t}:=\frac{\theta^{t}}{\sigma}
and
\bar{\theta}^{0}:=\frac{\theta^{0}}{\sigma}
.

C.3
.

Note that
we can rewrite the EM update rule as

\tanh(\nu^{t})=\tanh(\nu^{t-1})\cdot\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}\frac{\cosh(2\nu^{t-1})+1}{\cosh(2\nu^{t-1})+\cosh(2\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)}K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x.

Since
\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x=1

and
\cosh(2\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)\geq 1
, the EM update rule implies

|\nu^{t}|\leq|\nu^{t-1}|,\quad\operatorname{sgn}(\nu^{t})=\operatorname{sgn}(\nu^{t-1}).

If we take the
\ell_{2}
norm on both sides of the EM update rule for regression parameters, it follows that

\|\bar{\theta}^{t}\|=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x-\nu^{t-1})xK_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x|K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x=\frac{2}{\pi}

is bounded.

Proof.

As
\operatorname{SNR}:=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}\rightarrow 0
in previous Theorem, then coefficients in the EM update rule for
\theta
are as follows.

\displaystyle\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}\cdot\alpha_{\rho,\|\bar{\theta}\|,\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|,\nu^{\ast}}(\nu)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0^{2}\cdot\cosh(\nu^{\ast})\cdot K_{0}\left(\frac{|\nu|}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)=0

\displaystyle\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}\cdot\beta_{\rho,\|\bar{\theta}\|,\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|,\nu^{\ast}}(\nu)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0\cdot\operatorname{sgn}(\nu)\sinh(-\nu^{\ast})\cdot K_{1}\left(\frac{|\nu|}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)=0

Hence,
\theta^{t}
has no
\hat{e}_{2},\bar{e}_{2}
components, and it only contains the
\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t-1}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|}
component.

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}:=\frac{\theta^{t}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\cosh^{-1}(\nu^{\ast})}{\pi\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}\cdot\left\{\tanh(\nu)\ast\nu\left[\cosh(\nu^{\ast})\cdot K_{0}\left(\frac{|\nu|}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t-1}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|}\right]\right\}_{\nu\leftarrow\nu^{t-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t-1}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|}\cdot\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{t-1})\frac{\nu^{\prime}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\cdot K_{0}\left(\frac{|\nu^{\prime}|}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)\mathrm{d}\frac{\nu^{\prime}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t-1}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|}\cdot\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x-\nu^{t-1})x\cdot K_{0}\left(|x|\right)\mathrm{d}x

Note that
\tanh(\nu^{t-1}+\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)-\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)>0
for
\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|\neq 0,x>0
.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x-\nu^{t-1})x\cdot K_{0}\left(|x|\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}+\int_{-\infty}^{0}\right]\tanh(\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x-\nu^{t-1})x\cdot K_{0}\left(|x|\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\tanh(\nu^{t-1}+\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)-\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)\right)x\cdot K_{0}\left(|x|\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 0

Hence, we conclude that
\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t}\|}=\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t-1}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|}=\cdots=\frac{\bar{\theta}^{0}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{0}\|}
.

Consequently, we prove the closed-form expression for the EM update rule for
\theta
.

As
\operatorname{SNR}:=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}\rightarrow 0
in previous Theorem, the EM update rule for
\tanh(\nu)
is as follows.

\displaystyle\tanh(\nu^{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi\|\bar{\theta}\|\cosh(\nu^{\ast})}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\int}\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\nu^{\prime})K_{0}\left(\frac{|\nu^{\prime}|}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\right)\cosh\left(\nu^{\ast}\right)\mathrm{d}\nu^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|x)K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x

∎

C.4
.

(Corollary
3.3
in Section
3
: EM Updates in Noiseless Setting)
In the noiseless setting, namely
\operatorname{SNR}:=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}\rightarrow\infty
, the EM update rules for
\theta^{t},\tanh(\nu^{t})

at the Population level are

\displaystyle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\frac{2}{\pi}\left[\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right]

\displaystyle\tanh(\nu^{t})=\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right)\cdot\tanh(\nu^{\ast}),

where
\rho^{t-1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\varphi^{t-1}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|
.

Proof.

For brevity, we let
\rho:=\rho^{t-1},\varphi^{t-1}=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|
, thus
\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}=\cos\varphi^{t-1}

Denote
k:=\frac{\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|}
and

\alpha^{2}:=\frac{1}{(1+(1-\rho^{2})\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2})}\rightarrow 0_{+},x:=k\alpha^{2}\cdot\nu
, when
\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|\to\infty
.

Hence,
\sqrt{1+\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{2}}\sim\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|\sim\frac{\alpha^{-1}}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}
,

\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|^{-1}=k\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|^{-1}\sim k\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\alpha
.

Furthermore,
\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\nu)=\tanh\left(\nu^{t-1}-\frac{x}{k\alpha^{2}}\right)\rightarrow-\operatorname{sgn}(x)
as
\alpha\to 0_{+}
.

Evaluating the integral involving
K_{0}(|x|)
, we obtain the following expression as
\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|\to\infty
, namely
\alpha\to 0_{+}
.

\displaystyle\tanh(\nu^{t})=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to 0_{+}}\frac{1}{\pi\cosh(\nu^{\ast})}k\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\alpha^{2}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\int}\tanh(\nu^{t-1}-\nu)K_{0}(k\alpha^{2}\cdot|\nu|)\cosh(\rho k\alpha^{2}\cdot\nu-\nu^{\ast})\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{\pi\cosh(\nu^{\ast})}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\int}-\operatorname{sgn}(x)K_{0}(|x|)\cosh(\rho x-\nu^{\ast})\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{\pi\cosh(\nu^{\ast})}\int_{0}^{+\infty}2\sinh(\nu^{\ast})\sinh(\rho x)K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{\pi}\tanh(\nu^{\ast})\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\int_{0}^{+\infty}\sinh(|\rho|x)K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\tanh(\nu^{\ast})\left[1-\frac{2}{\pi}\arccos|\rho|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right)\cdot\tanh(\nu^{\ast})

Evaluating the integrals involving
K_{0}(|x|),K_{1}(|x|)
and the Lemma for the relations of unit vectors, we obtain the following expression as
\|\bar{\theta}^{t-1}\|\to\infty
, namely
\alpha\to 0_{+}
.

\displaystyle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\theta}^{t}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|}=\frac{1}{\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|}\cdot\frac{\theta^{t}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\to 0_{+}}-\frac{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\cosh^{-1}(\nu^{\ast})}{\pi}k^{2}\left(\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\alpha\right)\alpha^{3}\tanh(\nu)\ast\nu

\displaystyle\left[\cosh(\rho k\alpha^{2}\cdot\nu-\nu^{\ast})K_{0}(k\alpha^{2}\cdot|\nu|)\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}\cdot\frac{\bar{\theta}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}+\hat{e}_{2}\right)+\operatorname{sgn}(\nu)\sinh(\rho k\alpha^{2}\cdot\nu-\nu^{\ast})K_{1}(k\alpha^{2}\cdot|\nu|)\vec{e}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{(1-\rho^{2})}{\pi}\cosh^{-1}(\nu^{\ast})\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\operatorname{sgn}(x)x[\cosh(\rho x-\nu^{\ast})K_{0}(|x|)\hat{e}_{2}+\operatorname{sgn}(x)\sinh(\rho x-\nu^{\ast})K_{1}(|x|)\vec{e}_{2}]\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\rho^{2})}{\pi}\cosh^{-1}(\nu^{\ast})\left\{\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x|\cosh(\rho x-\nu^{\ast})K_{0}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x\right]\hat{e}_{2}+\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}x\sinh(\rho x-\nu^{\ast})K_{1}(|x|)\mathrm{d}x\right]\vec{e}_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\rho^{2})}{\pi}\left\{\left(2\left[\frac{1}{1-\rho^{2}}+\frac{|\rho|\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\right)}{(1-\rho^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right]\right)\hat{e}_{2}+\left(2\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\left[\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\right)}{(1-\rho^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{|\rho|}{1-\rho^{2}}\right]\right)\vec{e}_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\right)}{\frac{\pi}{2}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}(\vec{e}_{2}+\rho\hat{e}_{2})+\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}(\hat{e}_{2}+\rho\vec{e}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\right)}{\frac{\pi}{2}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}\cdot\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\frac{\theta}{\|\theta\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}\left[\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right]

∎

C.5
.

In the noiseless setting, the EM update rules at the population level for 2MLR are

\displaystyle M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim p(x)}|\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle x

\displaystyle N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim p(x)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim\mathcal{CAT}(\pi^{\ast})}(-1)^{z+1}\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle

In the noiseless setting, the EM update rules at the finite-sample level for 2MLR are

\displaystyle M_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t-1}\rangle x_{i}

\displaystyle N_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t-1}\rangle

Proof.

By letting
\sigma\to 0_{+}
, SNR
:=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}\to 0_{+}
, then
y\to(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle,\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\to\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle
.
Hence, these expressions are proved by taking the limits.
∎

C.6
.

In the noiseless setting, the easy EM update rule for
\theta
at the finite-sample level for 2MLR is

\displaystyle M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t-1}\rangle x_{i}

Proof.

This Lemma is proved by taking the limit
\sigma\to 0_{+}
for easy EM update.
∎

C.7
.

Let
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
and
\varphi=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|
, then the identity holds.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}|\theta^{\ast\top}xx^{\top}\theta|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\cdot\left\{\left[1-\frac{\arccos|\rho|}{\frac{\pi}{2}}\right]|\rho|+\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\cdot\frac{2}{\pi}(\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi)

Proof.

Decompose
x=\tilde{x}+\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
\tilde{x}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}
.

Let
\theta=\|\theta\|\left(\rho\hat{e}_{1}+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\hat{e}_{2}\right),\theta^{\ast}=\|\theta^{\ast}\|\hat{e}_{1}
and

\tilde{x}=\lambda_{1}\hat{e}_{1}+\lambda_{2}\hat{e}_{2}
, since
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

and
r=\sqrt{\lambda^{2}_{1}+\lambda^{2}_{2}},\lambda_{1}+\mathtt{i}\lambda_{2}=r\exp(\mathtt{i}\alpha)
and
\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y=r\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\alpha

Note
(\pi-2\arccos\rho)\rho
is an even function

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}|\theta^{\ast\top}xx^{\top}\theta|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}|\theta^{\ast\top}\tilde{x}\tilde{x}^{\top}\theta|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\mathbb{E}_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left|\lambda_{1}\left(\lambda_{1}\rho+\lambda_{2}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\underset{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{\iint}\left|\lambda_{1}\left(\lambda_{1}\rho+\lambda_{2}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right)\right|\frac{1}{2\pi}\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}_{1}+\lambda^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\left[\frac{1}{2\pi}\underset{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}{\int}r^{3}\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}}{2}\right)\mathrm{d}r\right]\cdot\left[\underset{[0,2\pi]}{\int}|\cos\alpha\cdot\cos(\alpha-\arccos\rho)|\mathrm{d}\alpha\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\cdot\frac{1}{\pi}\cdot\left[(\pi-2\arccos\rho)\rho+2\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\cdot\left\{\left[1-\frac{\arccos\rho}{\frac{\pi}{2}}\right]\rho+\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\cdot\left\{\left[1-\frac{\arccos|\rho|}{\frac{\pi}{2}}\right]|\rho|+\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta\|\cdot\frac{2}{\pi}(\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi)

∎

C.8
.

(Grothendieck’s Identity)
Let
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
and
\varphi=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|
, then the identity holds.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}\operatorname{sgn}\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\varphi

Note that
\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta\rangle\in\{1,0\}
is a Binomial random variable, hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta\rangle=+1\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{sgn}\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\varphi

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle x,\theta\rangle=-1\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{sgn}\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle\varphi

Appendix D
Proof for Results at the Population Level

D.1
.

(Proposition
4.3
in Section
4
: Recurrence Relation)
Assume the initial sub-optimality cosine satisfies
\rho^{0}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\neq\pm 1
, or equivalently
\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{0}|\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2})
.
Then the recurrence relation for EM updates at
population level characterized by the sub-optimality angle is

\tan\varphi^{t}=\tan\varphi^{t-1}+\varphi^{t-1}(\tan^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+1).

Proof.

As
\|\bar{\theta}^{\ast}\|\rightarrow\infty
, we can
obtain the EM update rule for
\theta^{t}
in previous Corollary
3.3
.

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\frac{2}{\pi}\left[\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right]

Let
\varphi:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, then since
\rho^{0},\rho^{t-1}
have the same sign
(validated by checking the sign of
\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle
)

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\left[\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right]

With
\left\langle\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right\rangle=|\rho^{t-1}|=\sin\varphi^{t-1}

\sin\varphi^{t}\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=|\rho^{t}|\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\rangle=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]

\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{[\varphi^{t-1}]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+2\varphi^{t-1}\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}

Therefore

\sin\varphi^{t}=\frac{\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}{\sqrt{[\varphi^{t-1}]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+2\varphi^{t-1}\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}}

Hence

\cos\varphi^{t}=\sqrt{1-\sin^{2}\varphi^{t}}=\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}}{\sqrt{[\varphi^{t-1}]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+2\varphi^{t-1}\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}}

Thus, we obtain the recurrence relation for
\varphi^{t}

\tan\varphi^{t}=\frac{\sin\varphi^{t}}{\cos\varphi^{t}}=\frac{\varphi^{t-1}}{\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}}+\frac{\sin\varphi^{t-1}}{\cos\varphi^{t-1}}=\tan\varphi^{t-1}+\varphi^{t-1}[\tan^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+1]

∎

D.2
.

(Proposition
4.4
in Section
4
: Cycloid Trajectory)
If
\rho^{0}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\neq\pm 1
, namely
\phi^{0}:=2\arccos|\rho^{0}|\in(0,\pi]
. Then the coordinates
\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t}
of normalized vector
\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}_{2}^{t}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}_{2}^{0},\forall t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
for EM updates at
the Population level can be parameterized with the angle
\phi^{t-1}:=2\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|\in(0,\pi]
as follows, where
\rho^{t-1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\mathtt{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}[\phi^{t-1}-\sin\phi^{t-1}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}[1-\cos\phi^{t-1}]

Hence, the trajectory of iterations
\theta^{t},\forall t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is
on the cycloid with a parameter
\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\pi}
, on the
plane
\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\}
.

Proof.

Let’s prove this, by using the recurrence relation in Proposition
4.3
.

Since
\tan\varphi^{t}=\tan\varphi^{t-1}+\varphi^{t-1}(\tan^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+1)
in Proposition
4.3
, it shows that
\tan\varphi^{t}\geq\tan\varphi^{t-1}\geq 0
, therefore
0\leq\varphi^{0}\leq\varphi^{1}\leq\cdots\leq\varphi^{t-1}\leq\varphi^{t}<\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Let
\hat{e}_{1}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
, and

\hat{e}^{t}_{2}:=\hat{e}_{2}\mid_{\theta=\theta^{t}}=\frac{\theta-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta}{\|\theta-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta\|}\mid_{\theta=\theta^{t}}=\frac{\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{t}\|}-[\rho^{t}]\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}}{\sqrt{1-[\rho^{t}]^{2}}}
and
\langle\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\rangle=0,\|\hat{e}_{1}\|=\|\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\|=1

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\langle\theta^{t-1}-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta^{t-1},\theta^{t}-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta^{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{t}\rangle-\langle\theta^{t-1},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\langle\hat{e}_{1},\theta^{t}\rangle\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}]-\sin\varphi^{t-1}[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{3}\varphi^{t-1}>0

Hence, we conclude that
\langle\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\rangle>0
, With
\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\perp\hat{e}_{1}
and
\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{t},\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\}\subset\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\}
,
\|\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2}\|=\|\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\|=1
, we validate
\hat{e}^{0}_{2}=\cdots=\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2}=\hat{e}^{t}_{2}
.

By the definition of
\hat{e}^{t}_{2}
, we obtain
\theta^{t}=\|\theta^{t}\|\{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\sin\varphi^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\cos\varphi^{t}\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\}=\|\theta^{t}\|\{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\sin\varphi^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\cos\varphi^{t}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}\}

Since
\theta^{t}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\}
, then

\theta^{t}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\}
, we can express

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}^{t}_{2}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}
.

Comparing the expressions for
\theta^{t}
, we derive the following result.

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}=\left\{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\sin\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\}\hat{e}_{1}+\left\{\cos\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}

With the recurrence relation
\sin\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}],\cos\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}
, which we showed in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, we
derive the implicit equation of
\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t}(t\geq 1)

\displaystyle\mathtt{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\hat{e}_{1}\right\rangle=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\hat{e}^{0}_{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\right\rangle=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}

Let’s cancel out the parameter
\varphi^{t-1}
in the parameterized curve

\varphi^{t-1}\mapsto(\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t})

\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{x}^{t}=\sqrt{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{y}^{t}\right)\left(1-\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{y}^{t}\right)}+\arccos\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{y}^{t}}

Let
\phi:=2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi\right)\in(0,\pi]
, then we rewrite the implicit equations of
\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t}(t\geq 1)

[
15
]
.

\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\mathtt{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}[\phi-\sin\phi]_{\phi=\phi^{t-1}}

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}[1-\cos\phi]_{\phi=\phi^{t-1}}

∎

D.3
.

(Proposition
4.5
in Section
4
: Quadratic Convergence Rate)
If
\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{0}|\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
, then the EM updates at population level satisfies

\tan\varphi^{t}\geq\frac{1+\sqrt{5}}{2}\cdot\tan\varphi^{t-1}.

Particularly, if
\varphi^{t-1}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|\in[\arctan 1.5,\frac{\pi}{2})
, then the EM updates at the Population level
satisfies

\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\tan\varphi^{t}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\geq\left\{\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\tan\varphi^{t-1}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\right\}^{2}.

Proof.

Let’s prove the inequalites in the Propostion
4.5
as follows.

Let’s define
a^{t}:=\tan\varphi^{t}
, then start from the recurrence
relation in Proposition
4.3
, note that
a^{0}>0
.

a^{t}-a^{t-1}=\arctan a^{t-1}([a^{t-1}]^{2}+1)>0

Firstly
, for the case of
\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{0}|\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
, since

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\arctan(a)}{\mathrm{d}a^{2}}=-\frac{2a}{(a^{2}+1)^{2}}<0
,
it is a concave function.

\frac{\arctan a^{t+1}-\arctan a^{t}}{a^{t+1}-a^{t}}>\frac{\mathrm{d}\arctan(a)}{\mathrm{d}a}\mid_{a=a^{t+1}}=\frac{1}{1+[a^{t+1}]^{2}}=\frac{\arctan a^{t+1}}{a^{t+2}-a^{t+1}}

Then for
\forall t\geq 0
, we obtain the following inequality.

\frac{a^{t+2}-a^{t+1}}{a^{t+1}-a^{t}}>\frac{1}{1-\frac{\arctan a^{t}}{\arctan a^{t+1}}}>1+\frac{\arctan a^{t}}{\arctan a^{t+1}}=1+\left[\frac{a^{t+2}-a^{t+1}}{a^{t+1}-a^{t}}\right]^{-1}\frac{1+[a^{t+2}]^{2}}{1+[a^{t+1}]^{2}}>1+\left[\frac{a^{t+2}-a^{t+1}}{a^{t+1}-a^{t}}\right]^{-1}

Hence, by solving
x>1+x^{-1}(x>0)
, we show that

\frac{a^{t+2}-a^{t+1}}{a^{t+1}-a^{t}}=\left(\frac{a^{t+2}}{a^{t+1}}-1\right)\left\{1+\frac{1}{\frac{a^{t+1}}{a^{t}}-1}\right\}>\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}\quad\forall t\geq 0

With
a^{1}-a^{0}=\arctan a^{0}([a^{0}]^{2}+1)>a^{0}
, we conclude that

\frac{a^{1}}{a^{0}}-1>1>\frac{\sqrt{5}-1}{2}
.

If we assume that
\frac{a^{t+1}}{a^{t}}-1>\frac{\sqrt{5}-1}{2}
,
then the inequality below shows that
\frac{a^{t+2}}{a^{t+1}}-1>\frac{\sqrt{5}-1}{2}
.

\left(\frac{a^{t+2}}{a^{t+1}}-1\right)\cdot\frac{\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}}{\frac{\sqrt{5}-1}{2}}=\bm{}\left(\frac{a^{t+2}}{a^{t+1}}-1\right)\left\{1+\frac{1}{\frac{\sqrt{5}-1}{2}}\right\}>\left(\frac{a^{t+2}}{a^{t+1}}-1\right)\left\{1+\frac{1}{\frac{a^{t+1}}{a^{t}}-1}\right\}>\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}

By mathematical induction,
\frac{a^{t+1}}{a^{t}}>\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}

for
\forall t\geq 0
. Therefore, we have proved the following inequality.

\tan\varphi^{t}\geq\frac{1+\sqrt{5}}{2}\cdot\tan\varphi^{t-1}\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

Secondly
, for the case of
\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{0}|\in\left(\arctan 1.5,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
, then we
have
a^{0}=\tan\varphi^{0}\geq 1.5
.

Applying the elementary inequality
\arctan a>\frac{\pi a}{2a+\pi}
,
\forall a>0
,
and noting the fact that
a^{t}\geq a^{0}\geq 1.5
, and
1.5-\frac{4\pi^{2}+\pi^{4}}{8(2\cdot 1.5+\pi)}+\frac{1}{8}\pi(4+\pi^{2})\approx 4.16>\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{\pi^{3}}{32}\approx 1.75

\displaystyle a^{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a^{t}+\arctan a^{t}(1+[a^{t}]^{2})

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle a^{t}+\frac{\pi a^{t}}{2a^{t}+\pi}\cdot(1+[a^{t}]^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2}[a^{t}]^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}a^{t}+a^{t}-\frac{4\pi^{2}+\pi^{4}}{8(2a^{t}+\pi)}+\frac{1}{8}\pi(4+\pi^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2}[a^{t}]^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}a^{t}+1.5-\frac{4\pi^{2}+\pi^{4}}{8(2\cdot 1.5+\pi)}+\frac{1}{8}\pi(4+\pi^{2})

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2}[a^{t}]^{2}-2\cdot\frac{\pi}{2}\cdot\frac{\pi}{4}a^{t}+\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{\pi^{3}}{32}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{\pi}{2}\left[a^{t}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]^{2}

Therefore, we have proved the following inequality.

\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\tan\varphi^{t}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\geq\left\{\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\tan\varphi^{t-1}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\right\}^{2}\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

∎

D.4
.

(Corollary
4.2
in Section
4
: Error of Mixing Weights
\pi^{t}
)
In the noiseless setting, the error of mixing weights for EM updates at the population level is

\|\pi^{t}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=\left|1-\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right|\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\right\|_{1}

where
\bar{\pi}^{\ast}:=\frac{1}{2}-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})(\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}),\varphi^{t-1}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|
and
\rho^{t-1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,

\rho^{0}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

Using Corollary
3.3
, and note that
\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})=\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})
, we obtain that equation.

\tanh(\nu^{t})=\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right)\cdot\tanh(\nu^{\ast})

Since
\pi^{t}(1)=\frac{1+\tanh(\nu^{t})}{2},\pi^{t}(2)=\frac{1-\tanh(\nu^{t})}{2}
and

\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(1)=\frac{1+\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\tanh(\nu^{\ast})}{2},\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(2)=\frac{1-\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\tanh(\nu^{\ast})}{2}
.

\|\pi^{t}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=|\pi^{t}(1)-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(1)|+|\pi^{t}(2)-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(2)|=|\tanh(\nu^{t})-\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\tanh(\nu^{\ast})|=\left|1-\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right|\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\right\|_{1}

In the above equation, we use such an identity
\tanh(\nu^{\ast})=\left\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\right\|_{1}
.
∎

D.5
.

(Theorem
4.1
in Section
4
: Population Level Convergence)
If the initial sup-optimality cosine
\rho^{0}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\neq 0
,
then with the number of total iterations at most
T=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|}\vee\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)
, the error of EM update at the population level is bounded by
\frac{\|\theta^{T+1}-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}<\varepsilon
, and
\|\pi^{T+1}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\varepsilon})\cdot\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\|_{1}

, where
\bar{\pi}^{\ast}:=\frac{1}{2}-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})(\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast})
.

Proof.

Let’s prove Theorem
4.1
, and consider the convergence rate of EM updates at
population level.

Let
a^{t}:=\tan\varphi^{t}
, and
\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{0}|\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
.

Step 1
. Determine the minmum iteration number
T^{\prime}
required to
ensure
a^{T^{\prime}}\geq 1.5

If
a^{0}\geq 1.5
, then
T^{\prime}=0
.

Otherwise
a^{0}<1.5
, with the inequality in Proposition
4.5
, we obtain the
following result for
\forall t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

a^{t}=\left[\prod^{t}_{t^{\prime}=1}\frac{a^{t^{\prime}}}{a^{t^{\prime}-1}}\right]\cdot a^{0}\geq\left(\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}\right)^{t}a^{0}

Hence, if
T^{\prime}\geq\left\lceil\frac{\log\frac{1.5}{a^{0}}}{\log\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}}\right\rceil
, we shows that
a^{T^{\prime}}\geq\left(\frac{\sqrt{5}+1}{2}\right)^{T^{\prime}}a^{0}\geq 1.5

Step 2
. Determine the minimum iteration number
T^{\prime\prime}
required
to ensure
a^{T^{\prime}+T^{\prime\prime}}>N_{\varepsilon}
, where
N_{\varepsilon}
is a big
number that replies on
\varepsilon
.

With the inequality in Proposition
4.5
, we obtain the following result for

\forall t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
t\geq T^{\prime}

\frac{\pi}{2}\left[a^{t+1}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]>\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\left[a^{t}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]\right)^{2}

Hence, if
T^{\prime\prime}\geq\left\lceil\frac{\log\left[\log\left(N_{\varepsilon}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)+\log\frac{\pi}{2}\right]-\log\left(\log\left(\frac{\pi}{4}\left(1.5-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\right)\right)}{\log 2}\right\rceil=\mathcal{O}(\log[\log N_{\varepsilon}])

\frac{\pi}{2}\left[a^{T^{\prime}+T^{\prime\prime}}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]>\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\left[a^{T^{\prime}}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]\right)^{2^{T^{\prime\prime}}}\geq\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\left[1.5-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]\right)^{2^{T^{\prime\prime}}}\geq\frac{\pi}{2}\left[N_{\varepsilon}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]

Hence
T^{\prime\prime}\leq\mathcal{O}(\log[\log N_{\varepsilon}])
, then
T:=T^{\prime}+T^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{a^{0}}\vee\log[\log N_{\varepsilon}]\right)
ensures
a^{T}>N_{\varepsilon}
.

Step 3
. Determine the relationship between
N_{\varepsilon}

and the desired relative error
\varepsilon
.

With Proposition
4.4
, we can write the expression for relative error of

\theta
as follows.

\displaystyle\frac{\|\theta^{T+1}-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}\sqrt{(\phi^{T}-\sin\phi^{T})^{2}+(1-\cos\phi^{T})^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}\left[\frac{\phi^{2}}{2}-\frac{\phi^{4}}{72}+\frac{\phi^{6}}{6480}-o(\phi^{6})\right]_{\phi=\phi^{T}}

By letting
\phi^{T}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)
, we
ensure that
\frac{\|\theta^{T+1}-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}<\varepsilon
.

\phi^{T}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)
, that is
\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi^{T}=\frac{\phi^{T}}{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)
.

With Corollary
4.2
, we show that
\|\pi^{T+1}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=\frac{2}{\pi}\left|\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi^{T}\right|\cdot\left\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\right\|_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)\left\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\right\|_{1}
.

Note that
\phi^{T}:=2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi^{T}\right)
and

a^{T}:=\tan\varphi^{T}=\tan\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\frac{\phi^{T}}{2}\right)
, expand
\tan\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-x\right)=\frac{1}{x}-\frac{x}{3}-\frac{x^{3}}{45}+o(x^{3})
.

a^{T}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)}-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)^{3}+o\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)^{3}\right)=\Omega\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)

Let
N_{\varepsilon}:=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)
, with
T=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\arctan a^{0}}\vee\log[\log N_{\varepsilon}]\right)
, we ensure
a^{T}>N_{\varepsilon}=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)
, then we obtain

T=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\arctan a^{0}}\vee\log\left[\log\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\right]\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\arctan a^{0}}\vee\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)

Note that
\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{0}|
, and
a^{0}:=\tan\varphi^{0}
, then
|\rho^{0}|=\sin(\varphi^{0})=\Theta(\varphi^{0})=\Theta(a^{0})
when
a^{0}<1.5
, thus

T=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{a^{0}}\vee\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|}\vee\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)

∎

Appendix E
Proof for Results at the Finite-sample Level

E.1
Upper-bound for Statistical Error

E.1
.

(Proposition
5.2
in Section
5
: Projected Statistical Error)
In the noiseless setting, the projection on
\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}
for the statistical error of
\theta
satisfies

\frac{\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right),

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
M_{n}(\theta,\nu),M(\theta,\nu)
are the EM update rules for
\theta
at the Finite-sample level and the
population level respectively, and the orthogonal projection matrix
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}
satisfies

\text{span}(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}})=\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}

.

Proof.

In the noiseless setting, the statistical error is

\displaystyle M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\right\}\tanh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t}\right)y_{i}x_{i}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}

Let
x_{i}=\tilde{x}_{i}+x_{i}^{\perp}
and
\tilde{x}_{i}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\},x_{i}^{\perp}\perp\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}
, we may assume
\dim\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}=2
without loss of generality, and decompose the space into
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}\oplus\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}^{\perp}
.

It implies that 2nd term of statistical error of (Easy EM) doesn’t depends on
dimension
d
of the space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

\displaystyle M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})=\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}^{\perp}+\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle\tilde{x}_{i}

Let the orthogonal projection matrix
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}
satisfy

\text{span}(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}})=\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}
, the 2nd term is the projected statistical error.

\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})]\|=\left\|\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle\tilde{x}_{i}\right\|_{2}

To estimate the projected statistical error, we begin with decompose it into two parts in terms of
\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}_{2}
.

Let
\tilde{x}_{i}=\lambda_{1i}\hat{e}_{1}+\lambda_{2i}\hat{e}_{2},\lambda_{1i},\lambda_{2i}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, so
\tilde{x}_{i}=\left\langle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\rho\\
\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\end{array}\right),\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lambda_{1i}\\
\lambda_{2i}\end{array}\right)\right\rangle\vec{e}_{1}+\left\langle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\\
-\rho\end{array}\right),\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lambda_{1i}\\
\lambda_{2i}\end{array}\right)\right\rangle\vec{e}_{2}
.

Let
Z_{i}:=|\lambda_{1i}|\cdot|\rho\lambda_{1i}+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\lambda_{2i}|
,

Z_{i}^{\prime}:=|\lambda_{1i}|\operatorname{sgn}(\rho\lambda_{1i}+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\lambda_{2i})\cdot(-\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\lambda_{1i}+\rho\lambda_{2i})
.

\displaystyle\frac{\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\frac{1}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\sqrt{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}])\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(Z^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z^{\prime}_{i}])\right|^{2}}

Note that both
Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime},i\in[n]
are sub-exponential with parameters
\left(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{4}},2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}\right)
(we explain the reason later).
By using the concentration inequality in
[
42
]
page 29,
equation (2.18) for iid sub-exponential r.v. with parameters, with
probability at least
1-2\delta

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}])\right|,\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(Z_{i}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{\prime}])\right|\leq\max\left(2^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}},2(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e})\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)

Hence, the proof is complete.

\displaystyle\frac{\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\max\left(2^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}},2(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e})\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\max\left(20\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}},45\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)

Now, let’s explain why both
Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime},i\in[n]
are sub-exponential. For brevity, we write
Z,Z^{\prime}
for
Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}
instead.

Z\leq\frac{1+|\rho|}{2}(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2})\leq\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2},\quad Z^{\prime}\leq\frac{1+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{2}(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2})\leq\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2},\quad|\mathbb{E}[Z]|\leq 1,\quad|\mathbb{E}[Z^{\prime}]|\leq 1

Let
W:=\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(2)
, then the
q
-th
moment of
|Z|
for
q\geq 2
,
\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{q}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{W\sim\chi^{2}(2)}[W^{q}]=2^{q}q!
.
By Minkowski’s Inequality and Stirling’s approximation,
-x\log x\leq\mathrm{e}^{-1}
and
\exp\left(\frac{\log q}{q}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{\log 3}{3}\right)=3^{\frac{1}{3}}
for
q\geq 2

and
\left\{\frac{2\sqrt{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\mathrm{e}}3^{\frac{1}{6}}+\frac{1}{2}\right\}\approx 2.907<2.91

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Z-\mathbb{E}[Z]|^{q}]^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{q}]^{\frac{1}{q}}+\mathbb{E}[|\mathbb{E}[Z]|^{q}]^{\frac{1}{q}}=\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{q}]^{\frac{1}{q}}+|\mathbb{E}[Z]|\leq[2^{q}q!]^{\frac{1}{q}}+1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\left[\sqrt{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{12}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}}{\mathrm{e}}\sqrt{\exp\left(\frac{\log q}{q}\right)}q+1\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\mathrm{e}}3^{\frac{1}{6}}q+1\leq\left\{\frac{2\sqrt{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\mathrm{e}}3^{\frac{1}{6}}+\frac{1}{2}\right\}q<2.91q

Then for
0\leq|\lambda|\leq\frac{1}{2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\{Z-\mathbb{E}[Z]\})]\leq\mathbb{E}[\exp(|\lambda||Z-\mathbb{E}[Z]|)]=1+\sum_{q=2}^{\infty}\frac{|\lambda|^{q}\mathbb{E}[|Z-\mathbb{E}[Z]|^{q}]}{q!}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{q=2}^{\infty}\frac{(2.91\mathrm{e}|\lambda|)^{q}}{q^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\frac{(2.91\mathrm{e}|\lambda|)^{2}}{1-2.91\mathrm{e}|\lambda|}\leq 1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}}(2.91\mathrm{e}|\lambda|)^{2}\leq\exp\left(\frac{\left(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\right)^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}\right)

Therefore,
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
(the same reason) are sub-exponential with parameters
\left(2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}(2\pi)^{-\frac{1}{4}},2\cdot 2.91\mathrm{e}\right)
.

∎

E.2
.

(Proposition
5.3
in Section
5
: Statistical Error)
In the noiseless setting, the statistical error of
\theta
for EM
updates at the Finite-sample level satisfies

\frac{\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\|_{2}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right),

with probability at least
1-\delta
,
M_{n}(\theta,\nu),M(\theta,\nu)
denote the EM update rules for
\theta
at the Finite-sample level and the
Population level.

Proof.

\displaystyle M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\right\}\tanh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t}\right)y_{i}x_{i}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}

Let
x_{i}=\tilde{x}_{i}+x_{i}^{\perp}
and
\tilde{x}_{i}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\},x_{i}^{\perp}\perp\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}
, we may assume
\dim\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}=2
without loss of generality, and decompose the space into
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}\oplus\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}^{\perp}
.

It implies that 2nd term of statistical error of (Easy EM) doesn’t depends on
dimension
d
of the space
\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})=\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle x_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}^{\perp}+\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle\tilde{x}_{i}

In the previous Proposition
5.2
, we bound the
\ell_{2}
norm of the second term with

\displaystyle\frac{\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})]\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\left\|\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\mathbb{E}_{\{x_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\right\}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle\tilde{x}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right),

Let’s focus on the first term, we start by rewriting the
\ell_{2}
norm of the first term in a different notation.

Let the projection matrix
P:=\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}+\hat{e}_{2}\hat{e}^{\top}_{2}
, select an orthonormal basis

\{\hat{e}_{3},\cdots,\hat{e}_{d}\}
to form
\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{\ast},\theta^{t}\}^{\perp}
, and let
Q:=(\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}_{2},\hat{e}_{3},\cdots,\hat{e}_{d})
.

Let
\left(\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime}{,x_{i}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\right):=Q^{\top}x_{i}
, where

\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}{,x_{i}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-2}
are
independent, and
\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{2}){,x_{i}^{\prime}}^{\perp}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d-2})
.

Note that
{x_{i}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}:=\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle Q^{\top}x_{i}^{\perp}=\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime},(1,0)^{\top}\rangle\operatorname{sgn}\left\langle\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime},\left(\rho,\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\right)^{\top}\right\rangle{x_{i}^{\prime}}^{\perp}
are indepent from
\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime}

and
\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime\prime}:=\langle\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime},(1,0)^{\top}\rangle\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1){,x_{i}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d-2})
.
We define
\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}:=\{\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in[n]}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}{,x_{j}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}:=\left\{{x_{ij}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}\right\}_{i\in[n]}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
for
j\in[d-2]
(Remark:
{x_{ij}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}
is the
j
-th
component of
{x_{i}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}
).

The components of projected vector
\alpha_{j}=\left\langle\frac{\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}}{\|\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}\|}{,x_{j}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}\right\rangle\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
are independent from each other,
and let
Z_{1}:=\left[\sum_{i\in[n]}(\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime\prime})^{2}\right]\sim\chi^{2}(n),Z_{2}:=\left[\sum_{j\in[d-2]}\alpha_{j}^{2}\right]\sim\chi^{2}(d-2)
are independent from each other.

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}|\langle\tilde{x},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x},\theta^{t}\rangle x^{\perp}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{n}\left\|\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime\prime}{x_{i}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}\right\|_{2}=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{n}\sqrt{\sum_{j\in[d-2]}\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime\prime}{x_{ij}^{\prime\prime}}^{\perp}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{n}\sqrt{\sum_{j\in[d-2]}\|\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}\alpha_{j}^{2}}=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{n}\sqrt{\left[\sum_{i\in[n]}(\tilde{x}_{i}^{\prime\prime})^{2}\right]\cdot\left[\sum_{j\in[d-2]}\alpha_{j}^{2}\right]}=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{n}\cdot\sqrt{Z_{1}Z_{2}}

By using the concentration inequality for Chi-square distribution (see Lemma 1, page 1325 in
[
29
]
), then with at least probability at least
\left(1-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)^{2}\geq 1-\delta

\displaystyle Z_{1}\leq\left(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)^{2}+\log\frac{2}{\delta},\quad Z_{2}\leq\left(\sqrt{d-2}+\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)^{2}+\log\frac{2}{\delta}

Therefore

\displaystyle\sqrt{Z_{1}Z_{2}}\leq 2\left(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)\left(\sqrt{d-2}+\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)=2\sqrt{n(d-2)}+2\log\frac{2}{\delta}+2(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d-2})\sqrt{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}

Note that
d\leq n
, hence we upper-bound the
\ell_{2}
norm for the first term.

\frac{1}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}|\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle|\operatorname{sgn}\langle\tilde{x}_{i},\theta^{t}\rangle x_{i}^{\perp}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{(d-2)}{n}}+2\frac{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{n}+4\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{n}}

Bound for Easy EM

For easy EM in the noiseless setting,
we show the following upper bound for the statistical error (in
\ell_{2}
norm) by combining the upper-bounds for the 1st and 2nd terms.

\displaystyle\frac{\|M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})-M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})\|_{2}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle\leq
